# Threat Model Report

## 1. System Overview

Parsed summary of the architecture input for the Brindle Freight Platform: a multi-agent platform that turns operator requests into planned, reviewed and audited shipment actions. 25 components were identified across 5 trust zones. The inventory below is the scope of this threat model.

### Components

| Component | Type | MAESTRO Layer | Description |
|-----------|------|---------------|-------------|
| client-portal | External Entity | L7 — Agent Ecosystem | Browser front end where operators submit shipment requests and approve agent proposals |
| partner-feed | External Entity | L7 — Agent Ecosystem | Carrier systems that push manifests and status events through signed webhooks |
| edge-gateway | Process | L4 — Deployment Infrastructure | Ingress proxy that terminates TLS, applies request limits and screens prompts before the intake agent |
| auth-broker | Process | L6 — Security and Compliance | Token broker that validates operator and partner credentials and mints short-lived session grants |
| intake-agent | Process | L3 — Agent Framework | LLM-backed agent that turns free-text requests into structured shipment tasks and answers operators |
| planner-agent | Process | L3 — Agent Framework | Planning agent that decomposes tasks, selects tools and assigns subtasks to the router |
| router-agent | Process | L3 — Agent Framework | Router agent that matches subtasks to worker agents and tracks their progress |
| worker-2 | Process | L3 — Agent Framework | Worker agent that optimises routes and looks up carrier rates through the tool server |
| worker-3 | Process | L3 — Agent Framework | Worker agent that drafts customs documents and delivery notices from retrieved context |
| reviewer-agent | Process | L3 — Agent Framework | Reviewer agent that checks worker output and presents proposals to operators for approval |
| tool-server-1 | Process | L3 — Agent Framework | Tool server that exposes carrier rate, booking, label and customs tools to the agents |
| model-gateway | Process | L1 — Foundation Model | Gateway that fronts the hosted foundation models and enforces per-agent quotas |
| embed-service-2 | Process | L1 — Foundation Model | Embedding service that vectorises documents and queries for retrieval |
| summarizer-llm | Process | L1 — Foundation Model | Fine-tuned summarisation model that writes customer-facing shipment summaries |
| vector-store-a | Data Store | L2 — Data Operations | Vector database holding embedded carrier documents and ticket history |
| kb-index | Data Store | L2 — Data Operations | Knowledge base index of carrier tariffs, customs rules and resolved tickets |
| memory-store | Data Store | L2 — Data Operations | Long-term agent memory that keeps task history and operator preferences |
| s3-archive | Data Store | L2 — Data Operations | Object archive of transcripts, exported manifests and fine-tuning corpora |
| feature-store-2 | Data Store | L2 — Data Operations | Feature store feeding the ranking and fine-tuning jobs |
| queue-a2 | Process | L4 — Deployment Infrastructure | Message bus that carries task and result messages between the agents |
| scheduler-1 | Process | L4 — Deployment Infrastructure | Scheduler agent that starts recurring planning runs and retries failed tasks |
| trace-collector | Process | L5 — Evaluation and Observability | Collector that stores prompts, completions and tool traces for debugging and replay |
| eval-runner | Process | L5 — Evaluation and Observability | Evaluation harness that replays traces against candidate models and grades answers with an LLM judge |
| policy-engine | Process | L6 — Security and Compliance | Policy engine that evaluates guardrail rules and approval requirements for agent actions |
| audit-ledger | Data Store | L6 — Security and Compliance | Append-only ledger of approvals, tool calls and administrative actions |

### Data Flows

| Source | Destination | Data | Protocol |
|--------|-------------|------|----------|
| client-portal | edge-gateway | Operator requests and session cookies | HTTPS (TLS 1.3) |
| partner-feed | edge-gateway | Signed manifest webhooks and booking confirmations | HTTPS (TLS 1.3) |
| edge-gateway | auth-broker | Token introspection requests | gRPC over mTLS |
| edge-gateway | intake-agent | Screened operator requests | HTTP/2 over mTLS |
| auth-broker | policy-engine | Entitlement lookups | gRPC over mTLS |
| intake-agent | queue-a2 | Structured task messages | NATS over TLS |
| intake-agent | embed-service-2 | Query text for retrieval | gRPC over mTLS |
| intake-agent | trace-collector | Prompt and completion traces | OTLP over TLS |
| scheduler-1 | queue-a2 | Recurring task triggers | NATS over TLS |
| queue-a2 | planner-agent | Task messages | NATS over TLS |
| planner-agent | model-gateway | Prompts and completions | HTTPS (TLS 1.3) |
| planner-agent | memory-store | Task history reads and writes | PostgreSQL over TLS |
| planner-agent | policy-engine | Action approval requests | gRPC over mTLS |
| planner-agent | router-agent | Subtask assignments | HTTP/2 over mTLS |
| planner-agent | trace-collector | Plan and tool selection traces | OTLP over TLS |
| router-agent | worker-2 | Route optimisation work orders | HTTP/2 over mTLS |
| router-agent | worker-3 | Document drafting work orders | HTTP/2 over mTLS |
| worker-2 | tool-server-1 | Tool calls (JSON-RPC) | HTTPS (TLS 1.3) |
| worker-2 | vector-store-a | Similarity queries | PostgreSQL over TLS |
| worker-2 | trace-collector | Worker traces | OTLP over TLS |
| worker-3 | tool-server-1 | Tool calls (JSON-RPC) | HTTPS (TLS 1.3) |
| worker-3 | summarizer-llm | Draft text for summarisation | gRPC over mTLS |
| worker-3 | trace-collector | Worker traces | OTLP over TLS |
| tool-server-1 | audit-ledger | Tool call records | gRPC over mTLS |
| summarizer-llm | reviewer-agent | Generated summaries | HTTP/2 over mTLS |
| edge-gateway | reviewer-agent | Approval queries and operator decisions | HTTP/2 over mTLS |
| reviewer-agent | audit-ledger | Approval records | gRPC over mTLS |
| policy-engine | audit-ledger | Policy decisions | gRPC over mTLS |
| embed-service-2 | vector-store-a | Embedding upserts | PostgreSQL over TLS |
| kb-index | embed-service-2 | Document batches for embedding | gRPC over mTLS |
| s3-archive | kb-index | Tariff and rule documents | HTTPS (TLS 1.3) |
| trace-collector | s3-archive | Archived trace batches | HTTPS (TLS 1.3) |
| trace-collector | eval-runner | Replay datasets | gRPC over mTLS |
| eval-runner | feature-store-2 | Evaluation features | PostgreSQL over TLS |
| feature-store-2 | summarizer-llm | Fine-tuning examples | gRPC over mTLS |
| audit-ledger | s3-archive | Ledger exports | HTTPS (TLS 1.3) |

### Technologies

| Category | Technology | Version (if known) |
|----------|------------|--------------------|
| Runtime | Python | 3.12 |
| Agent framework | Graph-based agent orchestration library | 0.2 |
| Gateway | Envoy proxy | 1.29 |
| Identity | OIDC with ES256 JWTs | RFC 7519 |
| Messaging | NATS JetStream | 2.10 |
| Database | PostgreSQL with a vector extension | 16 |
| Object storage | S3-compatible archive | unknown |
| Model hosting | Hosted foundation model API | unknown |
| Tool protocol | MCP over HTTP (JSON-RPC) | 2025-06 |
| Observability | OpenTelemetry collector | 0.98 |
| Front end | React single-page application | 18 |
| Policy | Rule-based policy engine | unknown |

## 2. Trust Boundaries

Trust zones group components that share a security posture. Each crossing between zones is a priority target for analysis.

### Trust Zones

| Zone | Trust Level | Components |
|------|-------------|------------|
| Public Internet | Untrusted | client-portal, partner-feed |
| Edge DMZ | Semi-trusted | edge-gateway, auth-broker |
| Agent Runtime Zone | Trusted | intake-agent, planner-agent, router-agent, worker-2, worker-3, reviewer-agent, tool-server-1, model-gateway, embed-service-2, summarizer-llm, queue-a2, scheduler-1 |
| Data Zone | Trusted | vector-store-a, kb-index, memory-store, s3-archive, feature-store-2 |
| Operations Zone | Trusted | trace-collector, eval-runner, policy-engine, audit-ledger |

### Boundary Crossings

| Crossing | From Zone | To Zone | Components | Controls |
|----------|-----------|---------|------------|----------|
| Public Internet to Edge DMZ | Public Internet | Edge DMZ | client-portal -> edge-gateway, partner-feed -> edge-gateway | TLS 1.3 termination, request size limits, webhook signature checks |
| Edge DMZ to Agent Runtime Zone | Edge DMZ | Agent Runtime Zone | edge-gateway -> intake-agent, edge-gateway -> reviewer-agent | Mutual TLS, token validation, prompt screening |
| Edge DMZ to Operations Zone | Edge DMZ | Operations Zone | auth-broker -> policy-engine | Mutual TLS, scoped entitlement lookups |
| Agent Runtime Zone to Operations Zone | Agent Runtime Zone | Operations Zone | intake-agent -> trace-collector, planner-agent -> policy-engine, planner-agent -> trace-collector, worker-2 -> trace-collector and 3 more | Mutual TLS, write-only trace and audit endpoints |
| Agent Runtime Zone to Data Zone | Agent Runtime Zone | Data Zone | planner-agent -> memory-store, worker-2 -> vector-store-a, embed-service-2 -> vector-store-a | Per-service database roles, TLS to the database, query allowlists |
| Data Zone to Agent Runtime Zone | Data Zone | Agent Runtime Zone | kb-index -> embed-service-2, feature-store-2 -> summarizer-llm | Mutual TLS, signed training manifests |
| Operations Zone to Data Zone | Operations Zone | Data Zone | trace-collector -> s3-archive, eval-runner -> feature-store-2, audit-ledger -> s3-archive | Scoped export credentials, TLS, bucket policies |

## 3. STRIDE Tables

One table per STRIDE category. Every row carries a delta status against the baseline run. RESOLVED findings are listed in Section 4c.

**Risk level computation (OWASP 3x3 matrix):**

| | LOW Likelihood | MEDIUM Likelihood | HIGH Likelihood |
|---|---|---|---|
| **HIGH Impact** | Medium | High | Critical |
| **MEDIUM Impact** | Low | Medium | High |
| **LOW Impact** | Note | Low | Medium |

### 3.1 Spoofing (S)

Threats where an attacker pretends to be something or someone else.

| ID | Status | Component | MAESTRO Layer | Threat | Likelihood | Impact | Risk Level | Mitigation |
|----|--------|-----------|---------------|--------|------------|--------|------------|------------|
| S-1 | UNCHANGED | client-portal | L7 — Agent Ecosystem | An attacker replays a captured session cookie against client-portal, which does not bind the credential to the session or channel it was issued for, so the replay is accepted as the original caller. The exposure follows from how client-portal receives operator requests and approval decisions from edge-gateway and hands them to the next stage without an independent check. A successful impersonation exposes every function the genuine caller may use and is hard to tell apart from normal traffic. Rated HIGH likelihood because the path needs no special position and uses interfaces that are exposed by design, and LOW impact because the effect is contained and easy to reverse once it is noticed. | HIGH | LOW | Medium | Bind each session cookie to the client certificate or device key, shorten its lifetime to minutes and reject any token presented from a different channel than the one it was issued to. Track coverage of this control as a release criterion for client-portal and record any exception with an expiry date. |
| S-2 | UNCHANGED | partner-feed | L7 — Agent Ecosystem | A compromised peer presents a forged service identity to partner-feed, and partner-feed accepts the claim without verifying the signature chain back to the issuing authority. The weakness sits at layer Agent Ecosystem of the stack, so every workflow that depends on signed carrier manifests inherits the exposure. Until the credential is revoked the attacker acts with the victim's authority, and the audit trail attributes the actions to the victim. Rated LOW likelihood because the path needs several preconditions to line up, such as insider access or a rare timing window, and HIGH impact because the affected data or action is central to shipment handling and hard to reverse. | LOW | HIGH | Medium | Require mutual TLS between edge-gateway and partner-feed and validate the full certificate chain, issuer and audience on every request instead of trusting the caller's own claim. Name an owner for this control, review it each quarter and route violations to trace-collector so that responders see them in one place. |
| S-3 | UNCHANGED | partner-feed | L7 — Agent Ecosystem | A shared or long-lived webhook signature on partner-feed lets anyone who obtains one copy authenticate as every tenant that uses the same deployment profile. Because partner-feed serves the Public Internet, a single failure here reaches each consumer of signed carrier manifests at once. A successful impersonation exposes every function the genuine caller may use and is hard to tell apart from normal traffic. Rated MEDIUM likelihood because the path needs a foothold on a neighbouring service or a prepared request, and HIGH impact because the affected data or action is central to shipment handling and hard to reverse. | MEDIUM | HIGH | High | Replace the shared webhook signature with per-workload identities that rotate automatically, and alert when one identity authenticates from two networks within the rotation window. Track coverage of this control as a release criterion for partner-feed and record any exception with an expiry date. |
| S-4 | UNCHANGED | edge-gateway | L4 — Deployment Infrastructure | A shared or long-lived bearer token on edge-gateway lets anyone who obtains one copy authenticate as every tenant that uses the same deployment profile. edge-gateway runs in the Edge DMZ and exchanges inbound API requests with reviewer-agent over HTTP/2 over mTLS, so anything able to reach reviewer-agent can also reach this weakness. Until the credential is revoked the attacker acts with the victim's authority, and the audit trail attributes the actions to the victim. Rated LOW likelihood because the path needs several preconditions to line up, such as insider access or a rare timing window, and HIGH impact because the affected data or action is central to shipment handling and hard to reverse. | LOW | HIGH | Medium | Replace the shared bearer token with per-workload identities that rotate automatically, and alert when one identity authenticates from two networks within the rotation window. Verify the control in a pre-production exercise and review it again after any change to edge-gateway. |
| S-5 | UNCHANGED | auth-broker | L6 — Security and Compliance | A shared or long-lived refresh token on auth-broker lets anyone who obtains one copy authenticate as every tenant that uses the same deployment profile. policy-engine trusts auth-broker to pass credential assertions and session grants along unchanged, which removes the second chance to catch the problem downstream. A successful impersonation exposes every function the genuine caller may use and is hard to tell apart from normal traffic. Rated MEDIUM likelihood because the path needs a foothold on a neighbouring service or a prepared request, and LOW impact because the effect is contained and easy to reverse once it is noticed. | MEDIUM | LOW | Low | Replace the shared refresh token with per-workload identities that rotate automatically, and alert when one identity authenticates from two networks within the rotation window. Confirm the fix with a targeted test against auth-broker, and keep the evidence with the change record for later audits. |
| S-6 | UNCHANGED | intake-agent | L3 — Agent Framework | Session fixation against intake-agent lets an attacker pre-seed an identifier and reuse the session after the victim authenticates. intake-agent runs in the Agent Runtime Zone and exchanges free-text shipment requests with embed-service-2 over gRPC over mTLS, so anything able to reach embed-service-2 can also reach this weakness. A successful impersonation exposes every function the genuine caller may use and is hard to tell apart from normal traffic. Rated MEDIUM likelihood because the path needs a foothold on a neighbouring service or a prepared request, and HIGH impact because the affected data or action is central to shipment handling and hard to reverse. | MEDIUM | HIGH | High | Issue a fresh session identifier on every authentication and privilege change, set strict cookie attributes and invalidate any identifier supplied before login. Verify the control in a pre-production exercise and review it again after any change to intake-agent. |
| S-7 | UNCHANGED | intake-agent | L3 — Agent Framework | Certificate or DNS spoofing between embed-service-2 and intake-agent lets an attacker terminate the connection and present a lookalike endpoint when pinning is absent. The weakness sits at layer Agent Framework of the stack, so every workflow that depends on free-text shipment requests inherits the exposure. Until the credential is revoked the attacker acts with the victim's authority, and the audit trail attributes the actions to the victim. Rated LOW likelihood because the path needs several preconditions to line up, such as insider access or a rare timing window, and MEDIUM impact because the effect is bounded to one workflow or one tenant and can be corrected with effort. | LOW | MEDIUM | Low | Pin the expected certificate authority for the link between embed-service-2 and intake-agent, validate host names strictly and fail closed when validation cannot complete. Name an owner for this control, review it each quarter and route violations to trace-collector so that responders see them in one place. |
| S-8 | UNCHANGED | planner-agent | L3 — Agent Framework | An unauthenticated callback or health route on planner-agent accepts requests that downstream code treats as if they had come from trace-collector. trace-collector trusts planner-agent to pass task plans and tool selections along unchanged, which removes the second chance to catch the problem downstream. A successful impersonation exposes every function the genuine caller may use and is hard to tell apart from normal traffic. Rated HIGH likelihood because the path needs no special position and uses interfaces that are exposed by design, and HIGH impact because the affected data or action is central to shipment handling and hard to reverse. | HIGH | HIGH | Critical | Remove implicit trust in route names: authenticate every route of planner-agent, including callbacks and health checks, and drop requests that carry no verified caller identity. Confirm the fix with a targeted test against planner-agent, and keep the evidence with the change record for later audits. |
| S-9 | UNCHANGED | planner-agent | L3 — Agent Framework | Session fixation against planner-agent lets an attacker pre-seed an identifier and reuse the session after the victim authenticates. Because planner-agent serves the Agent Runtime Zone, a single failure here reaches each consumer of task plans and tool selections at once. Until the credential is revoked the attacker acts with the victim's authority, and the audit trail attributes the actions to the victim. Rated MEDIUM likelihood because the path needs a foothold on a neighbouring service or a prepared request, and HIGH impact because the affected data or action is central to shipment handling and hard to reverse. | MEDIUM | HIGH | High | Issue a fresh session identifier on every authentication and privilege change, set strict cookie attributes and invalidate any identifier supplied before login. Name an owner for this control, review it each quarter and route violations to trace-collector so that responders see them in one place. |

The weakness sits at layer Agent Framework of the stack, so every workflow that depends on subtask assignments inherits the exposure. ### 3.2 Tampering (T)

Threats where an attacker modifies data or code without authorization.

| ID | Status | Component | MAESTRO Layer | Threat | Likelihood | Impact | Risk Level | Mitigation |
|----|--------|-----------|---------------|--------|------------|--------|------------|------------|
| T-1 | UNCHANGED | edge-gateway | L4 — Deployment Infrastructure | An attacker who can write to the channel between client-portal and edge-gateway modifies inbound API requests in transit because messages carry no integrity protection of their own. The weakness sits at layer Deployment Infrastructure of the stack, so every workflow that depends on inbound API requests inherits the exposure. Altered data is then treated as authoritative by every consumer, so the damage spreads before anyone compares it with the source. Rated LOW likelihood because the path needs several preconditions to line up, such as insider access or a rare timing window, and HIGH impact because the affected data or action is central to shipment handling and hard to reverse. | LOW | HIGH | Medium | Sign messages between client-portal and edge-gateway with a per-service key and verify the signature and a monotonic counter before accepting any change. Name an owner for this control, review it each quarter and route violations to trace-collector so that responders see them in one place. |
| T-2 | UNCHANGED | auth-broker | L6 — Security and Compliance | Backups and exports of credential assertions and session grants on auth-broker are writable by operators without a second approver, so a malicious change survives restores. auth-broker runs in the Edge DMZ and exchanges credential assertions and session grants with edge-gateway over gRPC over mTLS, so anything able to reach edge-gateway can also reach this weakness. Altered data is then treated as authoritative by every consumer, so the damage spreads before anyone compares it with the source. Rated HIGH likelihood because the path needs no special position and uses interfaces that are exposed by design, and MEDIUM impact because the effect is bounded to one workflow or one tenant and can be corrected with effort. | HIGH | MEDIUM | High | Protect exports and backups of credential assertions and session grants with write-once storage and dual approval for restore, and verify checksums against a separately held manifest. Add a regression test for this abuse path to the CI suite and alert through trace-collector when the control fires. |
| T-3 | UNCHANGED | intake-agent | L3 — Agent Framework | Replay of an earlier valid update to free-text shipment requests reaches intake-agent because updates carry no nonce or expiry, restoring a state the owner already corrected. intake-agent runs in the Agent Runtime Zone and exchanges free-text shipment requests with embed-service-2 over gRPC over mTLS, so anything able to reach embed-service-2 can also reach this weakness. Altered data is then treated as authoritative by every consumer, so the damage spreads before anyone compares it with the source. Rated LOW likelihood because the path needs several preconditions to line up, such as insider access or a rare timing window, and HIGH impact because the affected data or action is central to shipment handling and hard to reverse. | LOW | HIGH | Medium | Add a nonce and a short expiry to every update accepted by intake-agent, store seen nonces for the validity window and reject any repeat. Name an owner for this control, review it each quarter and route violations to trace-collector so that responders see them in one place. |

### 3.3 Repudiation (R)

Threats where an actor denies an action without the system being able to prove otherwise.

| ID | Status | Component | MAESTRO Layer | Threat | Likelihood | Impact | Risk Level | Mitigation |
|----|--------|-----------|---------------|--------|------------|--------|------------|------------|
| R-1 | UNCHANGED | client-portal | L7 — Agent Ecosystem | Operators approve changes to operator requests and approval decisions through a chat message or ticket comment that is not bound to the executed change, so approvals can be disputed. Because client-portal serves the Public Internet, a single failure here reaches each consumer of operator requests and approval decisions at once. Without reliable records an investigation cannot establish who acted, so disputes about responsibility stay unresolved. Rated MEDIUM likelihood because the path needs a foothold on a neighbouring service or a prepared request, and LOW impact because the effect is contained and easy to reverse once it is noticed. | MEDIUM | LOW | Low | Bind each approval to the exact change hash it covers, record approver and time in the audit ledger and refuse to execute unbound changes. Verify the control in a pre-production exercise and review it again after any change to client-portal. |
| R-2 | UNCHANGED | partner-feed | L7 — Agent Ecosystem | Clock drift between partner-feed and edge-gateway leaves events in inconsistent order, so the sequence of actions on signed carrier manifests cannot be reconstructed reliably. The weakness sits at layer Agent Ecosystem of the stack, so every workflow that depends on signed carrier manifests inherits the exposure. Without reliable records an investigation cannot establish who acted, so disputes about responsibility stay unresolved. Rated LOW likelihood because the path needs several preconditions to line up, such as insider access or a rare timing window, and MEDIUM impact because the effect is bounded to one workflow or one tenant and can be corrected with effort. | LOW | MEDIUM | Low | Synchronise partner-feed and edge-gateway to a trusted time source, record monotonic sequence numbers with every event and alert when drift exceeds one second. Track coverage of this control as a release criterion for partner-feed and record any exception with an expiry date. |
| R-3 | UNCHANGED | edge-gateway | L4 — Deployment Infrastructure | Log entries from edge-gateway can be edited or deleted by the same operators who administer it, so evidence of misuse can be removed after the fact. The weakness sits at layer Deployment Infrastructure of the stack, so every workflow that depends on inbound API requests inherits the exposure. Incident responders lose the sequence of events, which delays containment and weakens any later accountability claim. Rated MEDIUM likelihood because the path needs a foothold on a neighbouring service or a prepared request, and MEDIUM impact because the effect is bounded to one workflow or one tenant and can be corrected with effort. | MEDIUM | MEDIUM | Medium | Write the records of edge-gateway to an append-only store that operators cannot modify, and anchor periodic hashes of the log in a separate system. Name an owner for this control, review it each quarter and route violations to trace-collector so that responders see them in one place. |
| R-5 | UNCHANGED | edge-gateway | L4 — Deployment Infrastructure | Clock drift between edge-gateway and client-portal leaves events in inconsistent order, so the sequence of actions on inbound API requests cannot be reconstructed reliably. ### 3.4 Information Disclosure (I)

Threats where sensitive data is exposed to unauthorized parties.

| ID | Status | Component | MAESTRO Layer | Threat | Likelihood | Impact | Risk Level | Mitigation |
|----|--------|-----------|---------------|--------|------------|--------|------------|------------|
| I-1 | UPDATED | edge-gateway | L4 — Deployment Infrastructure | Secrets such as the bearer token for edge-gateway appear in process arguments and environment dumps that diagnostic tooling captures. Because edge-gateway serves the Edge DMZ, a single failure here reaches each consumer of inbound API requests at once. The exposed material can be reused for follow-on attacks and may trigger notification duties if it covers customer data. Rated LOW likelihood because the path needs several preconditions to line up, such as insider access or a rare timing window, and MEDIUM impact because the effect is bounded to one workflow or one tenant and can be corrected with effort. | LOW | MEDIUM | Low | Pass the bearer token to edge-gateway through a secret manager mount, exclude it from process listings and diagnostics, and rotate it after any exposure. Add a regression test for this abuse path to the CI suite and alert through trace-collector when the control fires. |
| I-2 | UNCHANGED | edge-gateway | L4 — Deployment Infrastructure | Verbose error responses from edge-gateway reveal internal paths, query text and identifiers of inbound API requests to any caller who can trigger a failure. Because edge-gateway serves the Edge DMZ, a single failure here reaches each consumer of inbound API requests at once. Disclosure is silent, so the affected parties learn about it only when the information is misused. Rated LOW likelihood because the path needs several preconditions to line up, such as insider access or a rare timing window, and HIGH impact because the affected data or action is central to shipment handling and hard to reverse. | LOW | HIGH | Medium | Return generic error codes from edge-gateway, log the detail on the server only and strip stack traces and identifiers from every external response. Add a regression test for this abuse path to the CI suite and alert through trace-collector when the control fires. |
| I-3 | UNCHANGED | edge-gateway | L4 — Deployment Infrastructure | inbound API requests held by edge-gateway are readable by any service in the Edge DMZ that shares the same network role, because access is granted by network position. Because edge-gateway serves the Edge DMZ, a single failure here reaches each consumer of inbound API requests at once. Disclosure is silent, so the affected parties learn about it only when the information is misused. Rated MEDIUM likelihood because the path needs a foothold on a neighbouring service or a prepared request, and MEDIUM impact because the effect is bounded to one workflow or one tenant and can be corrected with effort. | MEDIUM | MEDIUM | Medium | Grant access to inbound API requests per workload identity with least privilege, enforce it at edge-gateway and remove network-position trust within the Edge DMZ. Name an owner for this control, review it each quarter and route violations to trace-collector so that responders see them in one place. |
| I-4 | UNCHANGED | auth-broker | L6 — Security and Compliance | credential assertions and session grants held by auth-broker are readable by any service in the Edge DMZ that shares the same network role, because access is granted by network position. auth-broker runs in the Edge DMZ and exchanges credential assertions and session grants with policy-engine over gRPC over mTLS, so anything able to reach policy-engine can also reach this weakness. Disclosure is silent, so the affected parties learn about it only when the information is misused. Track coverage of this control as a release criterion for auth-broker and record any exception with an expiry date. |
| I-5 | UNCHANGED | auth-broker | L6 — Security and Compliance | Logs and traces emitted by auth-broker include raw credential assertions and session grants, so anyone with access to the logging pipeline can read content meant for one tenant. Rated MEDIUM likelihood because the path needs a foothold on a neighbouring service or a prepared request, and MEDIUM impact because the effect is bounded to one workflow or one tenant and can be corrected with effort. | MEDIUM | MEDIUM | Medium | Encrypt backups of free-text shipment requests with keys held outside the storage account, restrict the location to the recovery role and audit every read. Name an owner for this control, review it each quarter and route violations to trace-collector so that responders see them in one place. |
| I-45 | NEW | s3-archive | L2 — Data Operations | A timing or size difference in the responses of s3-archive lets an observer infer whether specific records of archived transcripts and training corpora exist. trace-collector trusts s3-archive to pass archived transcripts and training corpora along unchanged, which removes the second chance to catch the problem downstream. Disclosure is silent, so the affected parties learn about it only when the information is misused. Rated LOW likelihood because the path needs several preconditions to line up, such as insider access or a rare timing window, and HIGH impact because the affected data or action is central to shipment handling and hard to reverse. | LOW | HIGH | Medium | Equalise response size and timing for existence checks in s3-archive, and return the same status for present and absent records. Name an owner for this control, review it each quarter and route violations to trace-collector so that responders see them in one place. |
| I-46 | NEW | eval-runner | L5 — Evaluation and Observability | Verbose error responses from eval-runner reveal internal paths, query text and identifiers of replayed traces and judge verdicts to any caller who can trigger a failure. feature-store-2 trusts eval-runner to pass replayed traces and judge verdicts along unchanged, which removes the second chance to catch the problem downstream. The exposed material can be reused for follow-on attacks and may trigger notification duties if it covers customer data. Rated MEDIUM likelihood because the path needs a foothold on a neighbouring service or a prepared request, and LOW impact because the effect is contained and easy to reverse once it is noticed. | MEDIUM | LOW | Low | Return generic error codes from eval-runner, log the detail on the server only and strip stack traces and identifiers from every external response. Track coverage of this control as a release criterion for eval-runner and record any exception with an expiry date. |
| I-47 | NEW | policy-engine | L6 — Security and Compliance | Data in transit between audit-ledger and policy-engine is protected only on the outer hop, which leaves guardrail rules and approval decisions readable on the internal segment after termination. audit-ledger trusts policy-engine to pass guardrail rules and approval decisions along unchanged, which removes the second chance to catch the problem downstream. Disclosure is silent, so the affected parties learn about it only when the information is misused. Rated MEDIUM likelihood because the path needs a foothold on a neighbouring service or a prepared request, and HIGH impact because the affected data or action is central to shipment handling and hard to reverse. | MEDIUM | HIGH | High | Keep TLS end to end between audit-ledger and policy-engine, disable plaintext listeners and verify the negotiated protocol version in deployment checks. Confirm the fix with a targeted test against policy-engine, and keep the evidence with the change record for later audits. |

### 3.5 Denial of Service (D)

Threats where an attacker degrades or prevents legitimate access.

| ID | Status | Component | MAESTRO Layer | Threat | Likelihood | Impact | Risk Level | Mitigation |
|----|--------|-----------|---------------|--------|------------|--------|------------|------------|
| D-1 | UNCHANGED | edge-gateway | L4 — Deployment Infrastructure | A dependency of edge-gateway becomes unavailable and the failure cascades because edge-gateway waits on it synchronously for every request. Because edge-gateway serves the Edge DMZ, a single failure here reaches each consumer of inbound API requests at once. Operators lose the ability to book or amend shipments while the condition lasts, and recovery depends on manual intervention. Rated HIGH likelihood because the path needs no special position and uses interfaces that are exposed by design, and MEDIUM impact because the effect is bounded to one workflow or one tenant and can be corrected with effort. | HIGH | MEDIUM | High | Isolate calls from edge-gateway to the dependency with timeouts and bulkheads, serve degraded results where possible and alert on dependency health. Track coverage of this control as a release criterion for edge-gateway and record any exception with an expiry date. |
| D-2 | UNCHANGED | auth-broker | L6 — Security and Compliance | A single oversized or deeply nested payload makes auth-broker spend disproportionate memory and CPU parsing credential assertions and session grants. Because auth-broker serves the Edge DMZ, a single failure here reaches each consumer of credential assertions and session grants at once. The outage propagates to dependent agents, which stall or retry and prolong the disruption. Rated LOW likelihood because the path needs several preconditions to line up, such as insider access or a rare timing window, and MEDIUM impact because the effect is bounded to one workflow or one tenant and can be corrected with effort. | LOW | MEDIUM | Low | Cap payload size, nesting depth and parse time in auth-broker, and reject requests that exceed the limits before allocation. Name an owner for this control, review it each quarter and route violations to trace-collector so that responders see them in one place. |
| D-3 | UNCHANGED | auth-broker | L6 — Security and Compliance | Retry storms from edge-gateway multiply the load on auth-broker when it slows down, turning a brief delay into a sustained outage. Because auth-broker serves the Edge DMZ, a single failure here reaches each consumer of credential assertions and session grants at once. The outage propagates to dependent agents, which stall or retry and prolong the disruption. Rated LOW likelihood because the path needs several preconditions to line up, such as insider access or a rare timing window, and MEDIUM impact because the effect is bounded to one workflow or one tenant and can be corrected with effort. | LOW | MEDIUM | Low | Add jittered exponential backoff and a retry budget to edge-gateway, and enable a circuit breaker so that auth-broker can recover. Confirm the fix with a targeted test against auth-broker, and keep the evidence with the change record for later audits. |

The outage propagates to dependent agents, which stall or retry and prolong the disruption. Rated HIGH likelihood because the path needs no special position and uses interfaces that are exposed by design, and HIGH impact because the affected data or action is central to shipment handling and hard to reverse. | HIGH | HIGH | Critical | Enforce cost limits and pagination on queries to policy-engine, run heavy work on a separate pool and cancel queries that exceed the budget. Confirm the fix with a targeted test against policy-engine, and keep the evidence with the change record for later audits. |
| D-35 | UNCHANGED | audit-ledger | L6 — Security and Compliance | Expensive queries over approval and tool call records can be triggered by any authorised caller of audit-ledger, so one user can degrade the service for all. audit-ledger runs in the Operations Zone and exchanges approval and tool call records with reviewer-agent over gRPC over mTLS, so anything able to reach reviewer-agent can also reach this weakness. Operators lose the ability to book or amend shipments while the condition lasts, and recovery depends on manual intervention. Rated LOW likelihood because the path needs several preconditions to line up, such as insider access or a rare timing window, and LOW impact because the effect is contained and easy to reverse once it is noticed. | LOW | LOW | Note | Enforce cost limits and pagination on queries to audit-ledger, run heavy work on a separate pool and cancel queries that exceed the budget. Track coverage of this control as a release criterion for audit-ledger and record any exception with an expiry date. |
| D-36 | NEW | auth-broker | L6 — Security and Compliance | A slow-read attack holds connections to auth-broker open and ties up the workers handling credential assertions and session grants until none remain for real requests. The exposure follows from how auth-broker receives credential assertions and session grants from edge-gateway and hands them to the next stage without an independent check. Operators lose the ability to book or amend shipments while the condition lasts, and recovery depends on manual intervention. Rated MEDIUM likelihood because the path needs a foothold on a neighbouring service or a prepared request, and HIGH impact because the affected data or action is central to shipment handling and hard to reverse. | MEDIUM | HIGH | High | Apply idle and total timeouts on every connection to auth-broker, bound workers per client and move slow clients to a separate pool. Confirm the fix with a targeted test against auth-broker, and keep the evidence with the change record for later audits. |
| D-37 | NEW | worker-2 | L3 — Agent Framework | A slow-read attack holds connections to worker-2 open and ties up the workers handling work orders and route candidates until none remain for real requests. The weakness sits at layer Agent Framework of the stack, so every workflow that depends on work orders and route candidates inherits the exposure. The outage propagates to dependent agents, which stall or retry and prolong the disruption. Rated MEDIUM likelihood because the path needs a foothold on a neighbouring service or a prepared request, and LOW impact because the effect is contained and easy to reverse once it is noticed. | MEDIUM | LOW | Low | Apply idle and total timeouts on every connection to worker-2, bound workers per client and move slow clients to a separate pool. Verify the control in a pre-production exercise and review it again after any change to worker-2. |
| D-38 | NEW | summarizer-llm | L1 — Foundation Model | Retry storms from feature-store-2 multiply the load on summarizer-llm when it slows down, turning a brief delay into a sustained outage. feature-store-2 trusts summarizer-llm to pass shipment records fed into summaries along unchanged, which removes the second chance to catch the problem downstream. Operators lose the ability to book or amend shipments while the condition lasts, and recovery depends on manual intervention. Rated HIGH likelihood because the path needs no special position and uses interfaces that are exposed by design, and HIGH impact because the affected data or action is central to shipment handling and hard to reverse. | HIGH | HIGH | Critical | Add jittered exponential backoff and a retry budget to feature-store-2, and enable a circuit breaker so that summarizer-llm can recover. Verify the control in a pre-production exercise and review it again after any change to summarizer-llm. |
| D-39 | NEW | queue-a2 | L4 — Deployment Infrastructure | A single oversized or deeply nested payload makes queue-a2 spend disproportionate memory and CPU parsing task and result messages. The exposure follows from how queue-a2 receives task and result messages from scheduler-1 and hands them to the next stage without an independent check. The outage propagates to dependent agents, which stall or retry and prolong the disruption. Rated LOW likelihood because the path needs several preconditions to line up, such as insider access or a rare timing window, and HIGH impact because the affected data or action is central to shipment handling and hard to reverse. | LOW | HIGH | Medium | Cap payload size, nesting depth and parse time in queue-a2, and reject requests that exceed the limits before allocation. Track coverage of this control as a release criterion for queue-a2 and record any exception with an expiry date. |

### 3.6 Elevation of Privilege (E)

Threats where an attacker gains higher access rights than authorized.

| ID | Status | Component | MAESTRO Layer | Threat | Likelihood | Impact | Risk Level | Mitigation |
|----|--------|-----------|---------------|--------|------------|--------|------------|------------|
| E-1 | UNCHANGED | edge-gateway | L4 — Deployment Infrastructure | Object identifiers in requests to edge-gateway are trusted without an ownership check, so a caller can read or change inbound API requests that belong to others. The weakness sits at layer Deployment Infrastructure of the stack, so every workflow that depends on inbound API requests inherits the exposure. Once the boundary is crossed the attacker can reach administrative functions and persist there. Rated MEDIUM likelihood because the path needs a foothold on a neighbouring service or a prepared request, and LOW impact because the effect is contained and easy to reverse once it is noticed. | MEDIUM | LOW | Low | Check ownership or entitlement for every object reference at edge-gateway on the server, and deny by default when the check cannot be made. Add a regression test for this abuse path to the CI suite and alert through trace-collector when the control fires. |
| E-2 | UNCHANGED | edge-gateway | L4 — Deployment Infrastructure | Role claims carried in the bearer token presented to edge-gateway are honoured without confirmation against the authoritative directory. intake-agent trusts edge-gateway to pass inbound API requests along unchanged, which removes the second chance to catch the problem downstream. The caller gains rights that were never granted, which allows reading and changing data across tenants and approvals. Rated MEDIUM likelihood because the path needs a foothold on a neighbouring service or a prepared request, and MEDIUM impact because the effect is bounded to one workflow or one tenant and can be corrected with effort. | MEDIUM | MEDIUM | Medium | Resolve roles for edge-gateway from the authoritative directory on each request or from a short cache, and ignore role claims supplied by the caller. Name an owner for this control, review it each quarter and route violations to trace-collector so that responders see them in one place. |
| E-3 | UNCHANGED | edge-gateway | L4 — Deployment Infrastructure | An administrative interface of edge-gateway is reachable from the same network as ordinary callers and relies on a single shared password. Because edge-gateway serves the Edge DMZ, a single failure here reaches each consumer of inbound API requests at once. Once the boundary is crossed the attacker can reach administrative functions and persist there. Rated HIGH likelihood because the path needs no special position and uses interfaces that are exposed by design, and HIGH impact because the affected data or action is central to shipment handling and hard to reverse. | HIGH | HIGH | Critical | Move the administrative interface of edge-gateway behind a separate listener with strong authentication, network restriction and per-user accounts. Verify the control in a pre-production exercise and review it again after any change to edge-gateway. |
| E-4 | UNCHANGED | edge-gateway | L4 — Deployment Infrastructure | edge-gateway runs with broader permissions than its function needs, so a flaw in handling inbound API requests yields access to unrelated resources. edge-gateway runs in the Edge DMZ and exchanges inbound API requests with partner-feed over HTTPS (TLS 1.3), so anything able to reach partner-feed can also reach this weakness. Once the boundary is crossed the attacker can reach administrative functions and persist there. Rated MEDIUM likelihood because the path needs a foothold on a neighbouring service or a prepared request, and HIGH impact because the affected data or action is central to shipment handling and hard to reverse. | MEDIUM | HIGH | High | Reduce the permissions of edge-gateway to the minimum for inbound API requests, drop capabilities it does not use and review the grants on a schedule. Confirm the fix with a targeted test against edge-gateway, and keep the evidence with the change record for later audits. |

Once the boundary is crossed the attacker can reach administrative functions and persist there. Rated LOW likelihood because the path needs several preconditions to line up, such as insider access or a rare timing window, and MEDIUM impact because the effect is bounded to one workflow or one tenant and can be corrected with effort. | LOW | MEDIUM | Low | Derive the tenant for planner-agent from the verified credential, ignore client-supplied tenant headers and test cross-tenant requests in CI. Name an owner for this control, review it each quarter and route violations to trace-collector so that responders see them in one place. |
| E-33 | NEW | queue-a2 | L4 — Deployment Infrastructure | Object identifiers in requests to queue-a2 are trusted without an ownership check, so a caller can read or change task and result messages that belong to others. The exposure follows from how queue-a2 receives task and result messages from scheduler-1 and hands them to the next stage without an independent check. Once the boundary is crossed the attacker can reach administrative functions and persist there. Rated MEDIUM likelihood because the path needs a foothold on a neighbouring service or a prepared request, and HIGH impact because the affected data or action is central to shipment handling and hard to reverse. | MEDIUM | HIGH | High | Check ownership or entitlement for every object reference at queue-a2 on the server, and deny by default when the check cannot be made. Verify the control in a pre-production exercise and review it again after any change to queue-a2. |
| E-34 | NEW | eval-runner | L5 — Evaluation and Observability | A confused-deputy path lets a low-privilege caller ask eval-runner to act on replayed traces and judge verdicts using the privileges of trace-collector. The weakness sits at layer Evaluation and Observability of the stack, so every workflow that depends on replayed traces and judge verdicts inherits the exposure. Once the boundary is crossed the attacker can reach administrative functions and persist there. Rated MEDIUM likelihood because the path needs a foothold on a neighbouring service or a prepared request, and MEDIUM impact because the effect is bounded to one workflow or one tenant and can be corrected with effort. | MEDIUM | MEDIUM | Medium | Pass the caller's authority through to eval-runner and evaluate the original caller's rights, never those of trace-collector, for every delegated action. Verify the control in a pre-production exercise and review it again after any change to eval-runner. |

## 4. AI Threat Tables

Findings from the AI-specific agents. Each row adds an OWASP reference to the standard fields.

### 4.1 Agentic Threats (AG)

Threats arising from autonomous agent behavior, tool use and the way people rely on agent output.

| ID | Status | Component | MAESTRO Layer | Threat | OWASP Reference | Likelihood | Impact | Risk Level | Mitigation |
|----|--------|-----------|---------------|--------|-----------------|------------|--------|------------|------------|
| AG-1 | UNCHANGED | intake-agent | L3 — Agent Framework | Memory written by intake-agent is trusted on later runs, so one poisoned entry changes the behaviour of every future task. The exposure follows from how intake-agent receives free-text shipment requests from embed-service-2 and hands them to the next stage without an independent check. The agent acts at machine speed on behalf of the operator, so a single manipulated decision can cause many unwanted changes before a person notices. Rated HIGH likelihood because the path needs no special position and uses interfaces that are exposed by design, and HIGH impact because the affected data or action is central to shipment handling and hard to reverse. | ASI-06 | HIGH | HIGH | Critical | Tag memory entries with provenance and trust level, sanitise them on read and let operators review and expire entries written by intake-agent. Verify the control in a pre-production exercise and review it again after any change to intake-agent. |
| AG-2 | UNCHANGED | intake-agent | L3 — Agent Framework | intake-agent retries failed actions on its own, repeating side effects such as bookings or messages when the first attempt partly succeeded. intake-agent runs in the Agent Runtime Zone and exchanges free-text shipment requests with edge-gateway over HTTP/2 over mTLS, so anything able to reach edge-gateway can also reach this weakness. Actions taken by the agent look legitimate in the logs, which slows detection and complicates rollback. Rated HIGH likelihood because the path needs no special position and uses interfaces that are exposed by design, and HIGH impact because the affected data or action is central to shipment handling and hard to reverse. | ASI-08 | HIGH | HIGH | Critical | Make the actions of intake-agent idempotent with request keys, cap retries and require confirmation before repeating any action with external effects. Add a regression test for this abuse path to the CI suite and alert through trace-collector when the control fires. |
| AG-3 | UNCHANGED | intake-agent | L3 — Agent Framework | intake-agent can start further agents or processes on request, so a compromised prompt can multiply its reach beyond the intended scope. trace-collector trusts intake-agent to pass free-text shipment requests along unchanged, which removes the second chance to catch the problem downstream. The agent acts at machine speed on behalf of the operator, so a single manipulated decision can cause many unwanted changes before a person notices. Rated MEDIUM likelihood because the path needs a foothold on a neighbouring service or a prepared request, and HIGH impact because the affected data or action is central to shipment handling and hard to reverse. | ASI-10 | MEDIUM | HIGH | High | Remove the ability of intake-agent to start other agents unless an operator pre-registers them, and enforce a global ceiling on active agents. Track coverage of this control as a release criterion for intake-agent and record any exception with an expiry date. |
| AG-4 | UNCHANGED | planner-agent | L3 — Agent Framework | planner-agent runs without a budget on steps or spend, so a loop or an adversarial task keeps it acting long after it stopped being useful. The exposure follows from how planner-agent receives task plans and tool selections from router-agent and hands them to the next stage without an independent check. Actions taken by the agent look legitimate in the logs, which slows detection and complicates rollback. Rated MEDIUM likelihood because the path needs a foothold on a neighbouring service or a prepared request, and HIGH impact because the affected data or action is central to shipment handling and hard to reverse. | ASI-10 | MEDIUM | HIGH | High | Set hard limits on steps, tool calls, runtime and spend for planner-agent, stop on breach and report the stop reason to the operator. Verify the control in a pre-production exercise and review it again after any change to planner-agent. |
| AG-5 | UNCHANGED | planner-agent | L3 — Agent Framework | Delegation from trace-collector to planner-agent carries the full authority of the original session, so a low-trust sub-task can use high-trust rights. planner-agent runs in the Agent Runtime Zone and exchanges task plans and tool selections with trace-collector over OTLP over TLS, so anything able to reach trace-collector can also reach this weakness. The agent acts at machine speed on behalf of the operator, so a single manipulated decision can cause many unwanted changes before a person notices. Add a regression test for this abuse path to the CI suite and alert through trace-collector when the control fires. |
| AG-6 | UNCHANGED | planner-agent | L3 — Agent Framework | Memory written by planner-agent is trusted on later runs, so one poisoned entry changes the behaviour of every future task. planner-agent runs in the Agent Runtime Zone and exchanges task plans and tool selections with memory-store over PostgreSQL over TLS, so anything able to reach memory-store can also reach this weakness. Verify the control in a pre-production exercise and review it again after any change to planner-agent. |
| AG-9 | UNCHANGED | planner-agent | L3 — Agent Framework | Tool calls made by planner-agent are not tied to the end user, so the tool server cannot tell whose authority a request carries. ### 4.2 LLM Threats (LLM)

Threats to and through the language models: injection, poisoning, extraction, unsafe output and misinformation.

| ID | Status | Component | MAESTRO Layer | Threat | OWASP Reference | Likelihood | Impact | Risk Level | Mitigation |
|----|--------|-----------|---------------|--------|-----------------|------------|--------|------------|------------|
| LLM-1 | UNCHANGED | edge-gateway | L4 — Deployment Infrastructure | edge-gateway passes conversation history from earlier turns into the model unchanged, so an injection planted earlier keeps working in later turns. Because edge-gateway serves the Edge DMZ, a single failure here reaches each consumer of inbound API requests at once. The answers or side effects of the model then serve the attacker's goal, and the behaviour can be repeated against every user of the component. Rated MEDIUM likelihood because the path needs a foothold on a neighbouring service or a prepared request, and MEDIUM impact because the effect is bounded to one workflow or one tenant and can be corrected with effort. | OWASP LLM01:2026 | MEDIUM | MEDIUM | Medium | Re-screen conversation history on each turn in edge-gateway, expire old turns and mark which turns came from untrusted sources. Verify the control in a pre-production exercise and review it again after any change to edge-gateway. |
| LLM-2 | UNCHANGED | edge-gateway | L4 — Deployment Infrastructure | Prompt content logged by client-portal is later replayed to edge-gateway for evaluation, carrying planted instructions into a trusted context. The weakness sits at layer Deployment Infrastructure of the stack, so every workflow that depends on inbound API requests inherits the exposure. The answers or side effects of the model then serve the attacker's goal, and the behaviour can be repeated against every user of the component. Rated MEDIUM likelihood because the path needs a foothold on a neighbouring service or a prepared request, and MEDIUM impact because the effect is bounded to one workflow or one tenant and can be corrected with effort. | OWASP LLM01:2026 | MEDIUM | MEDIUM | Medium | Mark replayed prompts as untrusted when edge-gateway processes them, and exclude logged content from any privileged instruction slot. Track coverage of this control as a release criterion for edge-gateway and record any exception with an expiry date. |
| LLM-3 | UNCHANGED | edge-gateway | L4 — Deployment Infrastructure | An attacker splits a payload across several messages so that no single message trips the filter in front of edge-gateway. Because edge-gateway serves the Edge DMZ, a single failure here reaches each consumer of inbound API requests at once. The failure is probabilistic, so it may pass pre-release testing and appear only under adversarial input. Rated LOW likelihood because the path needs several preconditions to line up, such as insider access or a rare timing window, and HIGH impact because the affected data or action is central to shipment handling and hard to reverse. | OWASP LLM01:2026 | LOW | HIGH | Medium | Evaluate the accumulated conversation in edge-gateway as a whole, track cumulative risk and reset trust when it crosses a threshold. Name an owner for this control, review it each quarter and route violations to trace-collector so that responders see them in one place. |
| LLM-4 | UNCHANGED | intake-agent | L3 — Agent Framework | Encoded or multilingual payloads bypass the keyword filter in front of intake-agent, which only checks plain English text. queue-a2 trusts intake-agent to pass free-text shipment requests along unchanged, which removes the second chance to catch the problem downstream. The failure is probabilistic, so it may pass pre-release testing and appear only under adversarial input. Rated HIGH likelihood because the path needs no special position and uses interfaces that are exposed by design, and HIGH impact because the affected data or action is central to shipment handling and hard to reverse. | OWASP LLM01:2026 | HIGH | HIGH | Critical | Normalise and decode inputs before filtering in intake-agent, apply semantic checks instead of keyword lists and review filter misses weekly. Confirm the fix with a targeted test against intake-agent, and keep the evidence with the change record for later audits. |
| LLM-5 | UNCHANGED | intake-agent | L3 — Agent Framework | intake-agent passes conversation history from earlier turns into the model unchanged, so an injection planted earlier keeps working in later turns. ## 4a. Correlated Findings

Cross-agent correlation groups link findings from different agent categories that target the same component. Original findings stay in Sections 3 and 4; groups are additive.

| S-25 | UNCHANGED | Spoofing | — | embed-service-2 | L1 — Foundation Model | A shared or long-lived service credential on embed-service-2 lets anyone who obtains one copy authenticate... | Critical | Replace the shared service credential with per-workload identities that rotate automatically, and alert when one identity authenticates from two... |
| S-30 | UNCHANGED | Spoofing | — | scheduler-1 | L4 — Deployment Infrastructure | An attacker replays a captured scheduler credential against scheduler-1, which does not bind the credential... | Critical | Bind each scheduler credential to the client certificate or device key, shorten its lifetime to minutes and reject any token presented from a... |
| S-36 | UNCHANGED | Spoofing | — | eval-runner | L5 — Evaluation and Observability | Low-entropy identifiers accepted by eval-runner double as credentials, so an attacker who enumerates them... | Critical | Separate identifiers from secrets, generate secrets from a cryptographic random source of at least 128 bits and rate-limit failed lookups against... |
| S-39 | UNCHANGED | Spoofing | — | policy-engine | L6 — Security and Compliance | A compromised peer presents a forged service identity to policy-engine, and policy-engine accepts the claim... | Critical | Require mutual TLS between audit-ledger and policy-engine and validate the full certificate chain, issuer and audience on every request instead of... |
| T-10 | UNCHANGED | Tampering | — | tool-server-1 | L3 — Agent Framework | Configuration of tool-server-1 can be rewritten by any service that holds the shared deployment credential... | Critical | Keep the configuration of tool-server-1 in a versioned, access-controlled repository, require review for changes and alert on drift from the... |
| T-25 | UNCHANGED | Tampering | — | memory-store | L2 — Data Operations | Backups and exports of task history and operator preferences on memory-store are writable by operators... | Critical | Protect exports and backups of task history and operator preferences with write-once storage and dual approval for restore, and verify checksums... |
| T-27 | UNCHANGED | Tampering | — | s3-archive | L2 — Data Operations | Serialised objects arriving from kb-index are deserialised by s3-archive with permissive type handling... | Critical | Replace permissive deserialisation in s3-archive with a closed schema of allowed types, and reject payloads that name types outside it. |
| T-37 | UNCHANGED | Tampering | — | eval-runner | L5 — Evaluation and Observability | Serialised objects arriving from trace-collector are deserialised by eval-runner with permissive type... | Critical | Replace permissive deserialisation in eval-runner with a closed schema of allowed types, and reject payloads that name types outside it. |
| R-16 | UNCHANGED | Repudiation | — | worker-2 | L3 — Agent Framework | Operators approve changes to work orders and route candidates through a chat message or ticket comment that... | Critical | Bind each approval to the exact change hash it covers, record approver and time in the audit ledger and refuse to execute unbound changes. |
| I-31 | UNCHANGED | Information Disclosure | — | s3-archive | L2 — Data Operations | Data in transit between trace-collector and s3-archive is protected only on the outer hop, which leaves... | Critical | Keep TLS end to end between trace-collector and s3-archive, disable plaintext listeners and verify the negotiated protocol version in deployment... |
| D-8 | UNCHANGED | Denial of Service | resource_competition | planner-agent | L3 — Agent Framework | Expensive queries over task plans and tool selections can be triggered by any authorised caller of... | Critical | Enforce cost limits and pagination on queries to planner-agent, run heavy work on a separate pool and cancel queries that exceed the budget. |
| D-9 | UNCHANGED | Denial of Service | resource_competition | router-agent | L3 — Agent Framework | Retry storms from worker-2 multiply the load on router-agent when it slows down, turning a brief delay into... | Critical | Add jittered exponential backoff and a retry budget to worker-2, and enable a circuit breaker so that router-agent can recover. |
| D-34 | UNCHANGED | Denial of Service | — | policy-engine | L6 — Security and Compliance | Expensive queries over guardrail rules and approval decisions can be triggered by any authorised caller of... | Critical | Enforce cost limits and pagination on queries to policy-engine, run heavy work on a separate pool and cancel queries that exceed the budget. |
| D-38 | NEW | Denial of Service | — | summarizer-llm | L1 — Foundation Model | Retry storms from feature-store-2 multiply the load on summarizer-llm when it slows down, turning a brief... | Critical | Add jittered exponential backoff and a retry budget to feature-store-2, and enable a circuit breaker so that summarizer-llm can recover. |
| E-3 | UNCHANGED | Elevation of Privilege | — | edge-gateway | L4 — Deployment Infrastructure | An administrative interface of edge-gateway is reachable from the same network as ordinary callers and... | Critical | Move the administrative interface of edge-gateway behind a separate listener with strong authentication, network restriction and per-user accounts. |
| E-19 | UNCHANGED | Elevation of Privilege | — | tool-server-1 | L3 — Agent Framework | Multi-tenant separation in tool-server-1 depends on a request header that clients can set, allowing one... | Critical | Derive the tenant for tool-server-1 from the verified credential, ignore client-supplied tenant headers and test cross-tenant requests in CI. |
| E-23 | UNCHANGED | Elevation of Privilege | — | summarizer-llm | L1 — Foundation Model | Object identifiers in requests to summarizer-llm are trusted without an ownership check, so a caller can... | Critical | Check ownership or entitlement for every object reference at summarizer-llm on the server, and deny by default when the check cannot be made. |
| AG-1 | UNCHANGED | Agentic | — | intake-agent | L3 — Agent Framework | Memory written by intake-agent is trusted on later runs, so one poisoned entry changes the behaviour of... | Critical | Tag memory entries with provenance and trust level, sanitise them on read and let operators review and expire entries written by intake-agent. |
| AG-2 | UNCHANGED | Agentic | — | intake-agent | L3 — Agent Framework | intake-agent retries failed actions on its own, repeating side effects such as bookings or messages when the... | Critical | Make the actions of intake-agent idempotent with request keys, cap retries and require confirmation before repeating any action with external effects. |
| AG-9 | UNCHANGED | Agentic | — | planner-agent | L3 — Agent Framework | Tool calls made by planner-agent are not tied to the end user, so the tool server cannot tell whose... | Critical | Propagate a signed end-user context with each tool call from planner-agent and make the tool server authorise against it, not against the agent... |
| AG-13 | UNCHANGED | Agentic | — | router-agent | L3 — Agent Framework | An attacker chains two individually safe tools through router-agent to reach an outcome neither was approved... | Critical | Define forbidden tool sequences for router-agent, evaluate each call in the context of the previous ones and require approval for risky combinations. |
| AG-21 | UNCHANGED | Agentic | — | worker-3 | L3 — Agent Framework | The goal or instruction set of worker-3 can be changed through content it reads, steering it toward actions... | Critical | Keep the goals of worker-3 in signed configuration that content cannot alter, and refuse plan changes that originate from retrieved text. |
| AG-31 | UNCHANGED | Agentic | — | tool-server-1 | L3 — Agent Framework | The goal or instruction set of tool-server-1 can be changed through content it reads, steering it toward... | Critical | Keep the goals of tool-server-1 in signed configuration that content cannot alter, and refuse plan changes that originate from retrieved text. |
| AG-36 | UNCHANGED | Agentic | — | queue-a2 | L4 — Deployment Infrastructure | An attacker chains two individually safe tools through queue-a2 to reach an outcome neither was approved... | Critical | Define forbidden tool sequences for queue-a2, evaluate each call in the context of the previous ones and require approval for risky combinations. |
| AG-41 | NEW | Agentic | — | intake-agent | L3 — Agent Framework | intake-agent can act on free-text shipment requests without a human approval step, so a manipulated plan... | Critical | Require explicit human approval in intake-agent for actions above a defined impact threshold, and bind each approval to the exact action it covers. |
| AG-44 | NEW | Agentic | temporal_attack | scheduler-1 | L4 — Deployment Infrastructure | scheduler-1 can act on recurring planning triggers without a human approval step, so a manipulated plan... | Critical | Require explicit human approval in scheduler-1 for actions above a defined impact threshold, and bind each approval to the exact action it covers. |
| TE-2 | UNCHANGED | Agentic | trust_exploitation | intake-agent | L3 — Agent Framework | Persuasive phrasing from intake-agent pushes operators to approve proposals quickly, which reduces careful... | Critical | Neutralise urgency and pressure wording in intake-agent, present alternatives with their trade-offs and require a deliberate action to approve. |
| TE-11 | UNCHANGED | Agentic | trust_exploitation | reviewer-agent | L3 — Agent Framework | reviewer-agent adopts a persona that presents itself as a named colleague, so operators trust its... | Critical | Disclose that reviewer-agent is an automated agent at the start of each session and avoid human-like names or personal claims. |
